> help
Short walks, purchase lines and chats spend minutes within the hour; long acts finish the hour. Settings (standing, agency, rations) and queries take no time. Sleep keeps you in bed at least 4 hours (8 when you camp); wait to sleep on, or give any order (wake, drink, go) to get up.
Eat defaults to ONE serving; use eat hardtack 2 for two. Need effects settle once per hour; a partly spent hour uses its dominant activity.
Out on open ground with bedding, sleep makes camp where you stand; wait follows your lodging order, returning to a nearby paid or own bed when available.

hire <employer> [daily|weekly] | quit | dismiss <worker>
buy <item> [qty] | sell <item> [qty] | eat <item> [servings] [method] | cook <item> [servings] [method]
drink | fill <canteen/kettle> | empty <canteen/kettle> | fetch water | meal | sleep | wake | rest | work | wait <Nh|Nd> | wait until <HH:00|dawn|morning|noon|dusk|night|tomorrow>
Springs, creeks, rivers and fords are water: drink raw there and fill your vessels (a litre weighs a kilo: a fill stops where you could no longer walk, and empty pours a vessel out); fetch water walks to the nearest.

1855-04-01 00:00 | FAIR | Pratt & Daughter, Outfitters
Ellis Wayfarer — at leisure. Purse $50.00; household $0.00. 33°F, dry.
Food 6.0 days; Medium, 33.4 kg.
Vitals: Hunger 800; Rest 800; Thirst 850; Warmth 900
Health: Vigor 7/7; Wounds 0/12.
Counter closed; hours 6:00–21:00. Freight due Wednesday (in 3 days), every 3 days; sold out until then: Potatoes.
The Outfitter: general store, post-office counter, and the town's ledger of debts. The root cellar is the coolest storage in the village. Dried ginseng root and the odd bundle of furs are bought at the back counter and go down to Galena with the flour freight. Spring at Pratt & Daughter, Outfitters. Wheel ruts and footprints cross the softer ground. Augusta Pratt, Lydia Pratt are here.
Here: Augusta Pratt (#21), Lydia Pratt (#22).

You arrive at The Iron Ford (3 min on foot)
1855-04-01 00:00 | FAIR | The Iron Ford
Ellis Wayfarer — at leisure. Purse $50.00; household $0.00. 33°F, dry.
Food 6.0 days; Medium, 33.4 kg.
Vitals: Hunger 800; Rest 800; Thirst 850; Warmth 900
Health: Vigor 7/7; Wounds 0/12.
The ford where a cartload of iron bars lies in the creek bed, reset by the smith every spring. Teamsters say it keeps the wheels from sinking; the old women say it is for something else. Spring at The Iron Ford. The wind carries the smell of wet earth. The paths about The Iron Ford are quiet. Iron and running water mark a boundary here.
Here: no one else.

  Amos Whitcomb: “I once set a corner stone, and the owner moved it overnight. Six inches.”
  Lucius Vance: “Six inches?”
  Amos Whitcomb: “It was the best six inches in the county. I let him keep it.”
  Michael Dunleavy: “Shall I split a bit of kindling for you?”
  Amos Whitcomb: “Please do. I've been pretending the box was full.”
  Nora Dunleavy: “Don't repeat this, Mr. Whitcomb.”
  Amos Whitcomb: “When have I ever?”
  Nora Dunleavy: “Every time. But I tell you anyway.”
  Lucius Vance: “You remember everything, Mr. Pike. I envy it.”
  Joel Pike: “It's not a gift. It's a haunting.”
